### Fast Score
```
def fast_conv(a):
    n=len(a); M=2*n-1; L=1<<(M-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    return np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:M]
```
If a has negative tiny due numerical, clip before conv? For compute score use `a = np.maximum(a,0)` maybe.
```
def conv_score(a, include_high=True):
    a=np.asarray...; a=np.clip(a,0,1000); S=a.sum(); if S<1e-12: inf; c=fast_conv(a); return 2*n*c.max()/S^2
```
If target evaluator excludes some? We can filler. But for internal choose full. It matches shown. If actual hidden different, black-box choose final. But optimization using full may not minimize hidden if hidden differs? However hidden likely same formula just maybe different n? The problem as now coherent: full max. Good. ### LSE L-BFGS on softmax
Implement top-level functions:
```
def lse_softmax_func_grad(z, beta, n=None):
    z = z - np.mean(z)
    m = np.max(z); ez = np.exp(z-m); q = ez/ez.sum()
    # p conv q
    c = fast_conv(q)
    S? q sum 1
    p = c  # length M
    r = c.max()
    scaled = beta*(c - r)
    expv = np.exp(scaled)
    expsum = expv.sum()
    if expsum tiny: ...
    u = expv / expsum
    L = r + np.log(expsum)/beta
    val = 2.0*n*L
    # corr_j = sum_r q_r u_{j+r}
    # compute conv(q, u[::-1]) via FFT
    Mconv = 3*n - 2
    L2 = 1 << (Mconv - 1).bit_length()
    Q = np.fft.rfft(q, L2)
    Ur = np.fft.rfft(u[::-1], L2)
    full = np.fft.irfft(Q * Ur, L2)
    corr = full[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    avg = np.dot(u, c)  # sum u p
    grad = 4.0*n*q*(corr - avg)
    grad -= np.mean(grad) # remove flat if using centered z
    return val, grad
```
Check gradient factor for LSE: Objective val=2n L; grad wrt z = 2n * grad_L_wrt_z. grad_L_wrt_z = q*(2corr - 2avg)?? We had grad_L wrt q = 2corr. Then wrt z = q*(2corr - 2avg). Thus grad_L wrt z=2q(corr - avg). Times 2n -> 4n q(corr-avg). Good.

Test for small n by finite diff? Could be. Need ensure `avg` not 2? Wait corr_avg? derivative of L wrt z_j = ∑_i (∂L/∂q_i) (δ_ij q_j - q_i q_j) = q_j(∂L/∂q_j - ∑_i q_i ∂L/∂q_i). ∂L/∂q_j = ∑_k u_k ∂c_k/∂q_j = 2 corr_j. Sum_i q_i ∂L/∂q_i = 2 ∑_i q_i corr_i = 2 ∑_k u_k c_k = 2 avg. So grad_z = q_j (2corr_j - 2avg)=2q(corr-avg). Times val factor 2n =4n. Yes.

Potential issue: p=c for q sum=1, sum c=1. For beta small, u uniform, avg=1/M. corr_j = average of u over window times? corr=(1/M)*sum_{r=0}^{n-1} q_r? Since u=1/M, corr_j = (1/M)*sum q_r =1/M. avg=1/M. gradient zero for any q when beta=0! Indeed LSE with beta->0 constant, smooth objective is sum c? L=sum c /M =1/M constant, gradient zero. For small beta, gradient weak. Need start beta moderate, e.g. 50/2n? Since p ~1/n ~1e-4 for n7k, to focus peaks beta maybe 10000 or 100000. If beta small objective flat. We can use different beta schedule: start large? If beta very large, objective approximates max, gradient with weights only active peaks; L-BFGS may have near degenerate u. But active weights may vary. We can anneal from moderate (1000) to high (1e6). For p scale 1/n, typical p difference ~ (M-avg) maybe 0.5/n. βΔ ~ β/(2n). To see all peaks, βΔ ~1 -> β ~2n ~14000. To focus top, β up to 1e6. So schedule [2000, 10000, 50000, 200000, 1e6]. Use L-BFGS with maxiter maybe 200-1000.

But L-BFGS on softmax with high beta may be ill-conditioned. Need line search monotonic? Use options: ftol 1e-12, gtol 1e-8 maybe; maxiter 500; maxfun 5000. Since objective not convex? But should work.

Implementation of L-BFGS:
```
def run_lbfgs_softmax(q0, beta, maxiter=500, maxfun=2000, tol=1e-8):
    n=len(q0)
    q0=np.clip(q0,1e-12,1000); q0=q0/q0.sum()
    z0=np.log(q0); z0-=z0.mean()
    res=minimize(lse_softmax_func_grad, z0, args=(beta,n), method='L-BFGS-B'?? unbounded, jac=True, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxfun':maxfun,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-7,'maxls':20})
    z=res.x; z-=mean; q=softmax(z)
    return q
```
Use `method='L-BFGS'` (unbounded). Since no bounds. But L-BFGS requires finite gradient; good.
Could objective become nan if z large; softmax stable. If `expsum` overflow? scaled beta*(c-r) <=0; safe. If beta huge and c close r, okay. `np.exp` underflows others to 0; expsum maybe 1. Fine.

Need compute `avg = dot(u, c)`, but c length 2n-1; u same.

Performance: Each objective does two FFTs: one for p length L, one for corr length L2 (3n-2). If n=8192, L=16384, L2=32768. Each eval maybe 0.02-0.05s. 500 evals ~20s. Good.

### Exact active-set subgradient coordinate descent
We can write a direct subgradient/multiplicative descent that uses exact peaks, no LSE. This can polish after L-BFGS.

At current q sum1 nonneg. Compute c. Let target maybe exact max M. For active lags where c_k >= threshold (e.g., M*0.999 or all >M - tol?), compute gradient h_j = 2∑_{active} w_k q_{k-j}. Use weights w proportional to (c_k - lambda)_+ perhaps. Then gradient wrt log q: g_z = 2q(h - dot(q,h)). Update z += -step*g_z (since want decrease objective). Use line search: try step sizes decreasing until exact score improves, with nonneg implicit via softmax (q stays positive). But softmax update from z=add step? We can simply q_new = softmax(z - step*g_z) or simpler multiplicative: q *= exp(-eta*g_z), normalize. This keeps positive. Use step control. This is similar to gradient but exact.

Algorithm `multiplicative_peak_descent(q, n_iters, beta?`)
```
def multiplicative_descent(q, n_iters=2000, peak_frac=0.02, step=0.05, decay=0.999):
  q = np.clip(q,1e-12,inf); q/=q.sum()
  best=q.copy(); best_sc=score(q)
  eta=step
  for it:
    c=fast_conv(q); M=max(c); sc=2nM
    # choose active: c >= M*(1-tol) or c > M - delta
    act = c >= M - 1e-8*M? But exact max may have many. Use weight = max(c - M + delta*,0)? To smooth.
    W = np.maximum(c - M + threshold,0), threshold maybe max(0,0.01*(M - 1/M))? Or use LSE weights: u = exp(beta_active*(c-M)). Use beta_active=1/(M*(active width)). Simpler: use top K peaks with weights (c - M_topK_min)/range+1.
    # compute h = 2 corr(W/q? normalized? We need weights not necessarily sum1. If w sum=1 use previous. Let's normalize W sum=1.
    W/=sum(W)
    h = 2*corr(W, q)
    g = h - dot(q,h)
    # line search over eta: maybe log(q(1-epsilon*g?))? Since h may positive/negative.
```
But h_j = ∂(weighted max)/∂q? If W sum1, weighted avg p has gradient h. g_z = 2q(h - avg). Since avg=dot(q,h). To reduce weighted p, update z -= eta*g_z. For small eta. But sign? If peak high, gradient points to increase weighted p; we decrease z in direction gradient.

### Frequency sqrt projection
Let's design robust `sqrt_flat_project(q, alpha, n_iters?)`.
Input q length n sum1. We want update by target C. It is single-step, can be iterated with beta.

Pseudo:
```
def sqrt_flat_project(q, alpha=0.5, iters=1, flat_weight=0.0):
    n=len(q); M=2*n-1
    c=fast_conv(q)  # sum1
    flat = 1.0/M
    # target compression: T = c^alpha? To blend toward flat:
    eps=1e-15
    # T = c if alpha=1; flat if alpha=0
    T = np.power(c + eps, alpha)
    # But if alpha=0, np.power gives 1, good. For zeros? eps.
    T = T / T.sum()
    # Optional blend: T=(1-lam)*c + lam*flat
    # Actually power with normalized preserves support; alpha=0 flat too.
    # Use target sum 1.
    # FFT length L = next pow2 >= M
    L = 1 << (M-1).bit_length()
    Cfreq = np.fft.rfft(T, L)
    # ensure Cfreq[0]=sqrt? We want Bfreq^2 = Cfreq. Bfreq = sqrt(Cfreq)
    Bfreq = np.sqrt(Cfreq)  # complex
    # enforce Hermitian/self-conj:
    # rfft doesn't let symmetrize directly? We'll use full fft for safety.
```
Maybe use full FFT to symmetrize:
```
    Cf = np.fft.fft(T, L)
    Bf = np.sqrt(Cf)
    # Force Bf[0] real positive, avoid negative DC? Cf[0]=sumT=1 positive. Bf[0]=1.
    Bf[0] = abs(Bf[0])
    if L % 2 == 0:
        # Nyquist must be real
        Bf[L//2] = abs(Bf[L//2])  # but if Cf[Nyq] negative, abs loses; but better real
    # symmetrize: Bf_new[k] = 0.5*(Bf[k] + conj(Bf[-k mod L]))
    idx = np.arange(L)
    inv = (-idx) % L
    Bf = 0.5*(Bf + np.conj(Bf[inv]))
    b = np.fft.ifft(Bf).real
    # We only need first n coefficients; but due circular wrap, b might have wrap components.
    # Since we want length n support, take first n? But if there is mass after n, normalizing first n changes square.
    # We can also fold b modulo? no.
    q_new = np.maximum(b[:n], 0)
    S = q_new.sum()
    if S < 1e-12: return q
    q_new /= S
    return q_new
```
But taking first n and clipping may be too destructive. Alternative: choose L = 2n? b length L; if polynomial square support 2n-1, ideal b length n and zero from n to L-1. The energy beyond n indicates error. We can project by zeroing beyond n and renormalizing. This is an alternating projection.

Need ensure `sqrt` phase branch: Since we symmetrize by averaging with conjugate, `Bf^2` no longer equal Cf exactly, but okay. For DC small positive. Could choose `Bf[0]=abs(Cf[0])**0.5` =1. If `Cf` has zeros, sqrt fine.

Parameterization: We can choose target T as combination of current c and flat:
`T = (1 - μ) * c_norm + μ * flat`, where μ anneals from 0 to 1? If μ=1 flat. But if use flat too strong, sqrt may produce poor q. Start μ small, increase. Or T = c^α / sum with α<1. Power target with α=0 flat; α=1 current. `α` anneal from 0.8 to 0.1.
Maybe use `T = (1-λ)*c + λ*flat` safer (no power with zeros). For reducing peaks, we can do `T = c.copy(); T[T>m] = m` then normalize to sum1 (peaks clipped) plus increase lows? Clipping only distributes peak energy to all? If normalize after clipping, lows also shrink. Could better: set T = c; after clipping peaks to M_new, deficit D = sum clipped - ...; add D uniformly to all TK: T = min(c, M_new); deficit = 1 - sum(T); T += deficit / M_count; if deficit positive; if deficit negative? Need ensure peak level chosen so sum T=1. This is bang-bang target: flatten peaks at level L and raise lows. But sqrt of such target may be too complex.
Simpler: `T = c ** alpha` tends to raise lows more. Use alpha from 1 to 0.

Could use multiple `sqrt_project` from different initializations to escape. We can include as a function returning updated q, and a loop with acceptance (only if exact score improves). It may produce large changes; line search blending: `q_new = (1-t)q + t*proj`, for t from 1 down to 0.01, accept if score improves. This makes it safe.

### LP / dual direction
The previous state-of-art LP: for current f with M, solve maximize sum b (b>=0) subject to f*b <= M. This gives a feasible direction in convex set defined by current f as a condition. Then rescale and update. This is a kind of "ice ray". Can we implement efficiently? For current f, constraints (f*b)_k <= M for k=0..2n-2. Matrix is convolution (convolution with f) of size M x n. For n~7k, M~14k, too large for LP solvers. CVXPY maybe too big. Previous claimed used LP; maybe they used active set or scipy linprog? Too slow. We can approximate by solving KKT: maximize ∑b subject to nonneg and linear constraints. At optimum, active constraints. Could use projected gradient / water-filling. But maybe not needed.

Alternatively, we can solve a *convex* quadratic program for next g: minimize (g*g)_∞? Nonconvex. Or approximate LP using dual variables from C: Given active weights u (from LSE), maximize sum b subject to ∑ u_k (f*b)_k <= M∑u? This is linear with separable costs: h_j = ∑_k u_k f_{k-j}; constraint? Wait if use weights, objective linear, constraint weighted sum <= M. Extreme point would make h_j * b_j? def recursive_flat_init(n, c_factor=1.0):
   b = np.zeros(n)
   c = c_factor / (2*n-1) # target lower coefficients?
   b[0] = sqrt(max(c,0))
   for k in range(1,n):
       known = 0.0
       # C_k target maybe c (or current)
       # known = sum_{i=0}^{k-1} b_i b_{k-i}, but terms with i and k-i; if k-i<k.

def resample_to_length(a, n_new):
    old = np.asarray(a,float); m=len(old)
    if m==n_new: return old
    x_old = np.arange(m)
    x_new = np.linspace(0, m-1, n_new)  # or scale to same interval?
    # If step function intervals, maybe interpolation with circular? Use np.interp.
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, old, left=0, right=0)

### Candidate generation for a length
We can create a list of initial q for given n and parent:
```
def make_initializations(n, rng, parent):
  x = arange(n)/n
  inits=[]
  if parent: p=resample(parent,n); clip; normalize; if sum>0 inits.append(p)
  inits.append(np.ones(n)/n)
  # U-shape: (i+eps)^-a*(n-1-i+eps)^-b
  for a in [0.25,0.5,0.75]:
      left = (arange(n)+0.5)**(-a)
      right = (n-1-arange(n)+0.5)**(-a)
      q=left*right; normalize
  # Cosine mid/edge
  q = np.abs(np.cos(np.pi*x))**p + eps
  # Edge enhanced: 0.2+0.8*abs(2x-1)
  # Two lobes: cos^2(pi(x-0.25))^2 + ...
  # Random smooth positive: sum cos freq 1..5 with small amplitudes
  return inits
```
We should keep all positive (eps) to log.

### Multiplicative Descent details
Let's design robust `multiplicative_descent(q0, n_iters, beta_exact, step, decay, max_step, tol, time_limit=None)`.
Pseudo:
```
def multiplicative_descent(q0, n_iters=2000, beta=2000.0, step=0.05, decay=0.9999, max_step=0.2, n_top=None, time_limit=None, report_every=200):
    n=len(q0); q=np.clip(q0,1e-12,None); q/=q.sum()
    z=np.log(q); z-=np.mean(z)
    best=q.copy(); best_sc=conv_score(q)
    eta=step
    for it in range(n_iters):
       if time_limit and time.monotonic()>time_limit: break
       c=fast_conv(q); M=c.max(); sc=2*n*M  # q sum1
       if sc<best_sc: best_sc=sc; best=q.copy()
       # weights u using LSE around top? Use beta weights on peaks relative to M.
       scaled = beta*(c-M)
       # To avoid underflow if beta large and M small, exp ok.
       expv=np.exp(scaled); s=expv.sum(); if s<1e-300: u=np.zeros; u[argmax]=1
       else u=expv/s
       h=corr_weighted(q,u)  # ∑ u_k q_{k-j}
       avg=float(np.dot(u,c))
       # gradient wrt log q for objective 2n*(avg? Actually weights sum1, objective avg; grad = 2 q (h-avg)
       g=2*q*(h-avg)
       g-=np.mean(g)? For softmax update, to preserve scale, if we update z, gradient can have mean. But if we simply multiplicative normalize, we can ignore.
       # Direction: new z = z - eta*g. But g has mean? For large peak, some positive/negative. Normalize g to norm.
       gn=np.linalg.norm(g)
       if gn < 1e-15: break
       accepted=False
       trial_eta=eta
       for _ in range(12):
           if trial_eta < 1e-12: break
           z_new = z - trial_eta*g/gn  # normalize direction
           q_new=softmax(z_new)
           sc_new=conv_score(q_new)
           if sc_new < sc - 1e-12:
              z=z_new; q=q_new; sc=sc_new
              if sc<best_sc: best_sc=sc; best=q.copy()
              accepted=True
              break
           trial_eta *= 0.5
       if not accepted:
           # Try very small or random perturbation? shrink eta
           eta *= 0.5
       else:
           # maybe increase eta slightly if accepted small
           # if trial_eta*? not know. Keep eta *= max(decay,?)
           eta = min(max_step, eta*1.02) # or decay slowly
       # optional: if no many improvements, use exact top peaks only? beta high.
    return best, best_sc
```
But we used `g/gn` making step dimensionless. Could be too large. Alternative use raw g with adaptive step. For gradient of objective scale ~? q ~1/n, h~1/n, avg~1/n, g~1/n^2? Norm ~√n/n^2=1/n^{3/2} ~ 1/(7k^1.5)=4e-6. Objective gradient scale? If use raw, step maybe 1e6. Normalizing direction and step small may be okay. But LSE gradient from objective had factor 4n maybe scale ~1/√n. Let's not normalize g to unit? Use raw `g` and step ~0.01. For beta weights, gradient magnitude small. Could compute `g = 2*q*(h-avg)`; normalize q; norm ~? h ~ sum u q ~ if one peak, h_j ~ q_{...} ~1/n. avg ~1/n. Difference maybe up to 1/n. q times difference ~1/n^2. Norm over n ~1/n^{1.5}. For n=7000, 4e-6. To change q significantly, eta*g norm effect: z change eta*4e-6; to get z change 0.1, eta~25000. So if multiply step 0.05 after g not normalized, no effect. We can define direction `d = q*(h-avg)` and normalize `d_norm=np.linalg.norm(d)` and use step in z units (e.g., 0.1). So:
```
d = q*(h-avg)
dn = norm(d)
delta = -eta*d/dn
```
Then eta ~0.01-0.5. Good.
Need choose sign: If peak high, objective avg = ∑ u c. We want decrease. For a variable that contributes to peak, h_j high; d positive for those; subtract d lowers q_j. Negative variables (not contributing) increase? q can increase where h<avg. Good.
Line search with z update.

But softmax update may change sum and all q; if d has mean not zero, softmax will shift all? Since z update contains constant component; adding constant to z doesn't change q, so we can center d to improve. `d -= np.mean(d)` before normalizing. That isolates shape changes. Then q changes. Good.

So multiplicative algorithm:
- Compute u weights; d = q*(h - avg); d = d - mean(d); if norm small break.
- Try step eta (z-scale) around 0.05 maybe; z_new = z - eta*d/ndn; q_new=softmax; accept if score improves. If accept, set eta = min(max, eta*1.05) else eta*=0.5.
- update z each accept.
- For exactness, increase beta over iterations from 500 to 1e6 to focus.
- Optionally after some iterations, do exact top K weights: u on top K peaks with weights by excess; may be faster.

Could be too slow if each line search 12 convs. 2000*12=24k convs; each conv 16k FFT ~0.01s ->240s; okay. ### Let's think of more mathematically targeted algorithm
We want minimize square coefficient max. This is equivalent to designing a nonnegative "probability distribution" whose self-convolution is flat. There is a known transformation using Kac's? Maybe we can formulate via nonnegative trigonometric polynomials. Let A(θ)=∑ q_i e^{-iθi}. We want |? The coefficients of A(θ)^2. The maximum coefficient can be expressed via Fourier series: c_k = (1/L)∫ e^{ikθ} A(θ)^2 dθ. To bound all c_k, A(θ)^2 must have small Fourier coefficients except maybe DC? Actually all c_k are Fourier coefficients of A(θ)^2 (length L), not magnitude. If A(θ)^2 = γ(θ) where its coefficients flat 1/M for k=0..M-1, then for k outside support zero. This is a sparse positive? A^2 has nonnegative real time coefficients but complex frequency. Nonnegativity of q imposes A is a polynomial with nonnegative coefficients. There are approximation methods: choose A(θ) = e^{-iφ(θ)} e^{ψ(θ)} with constraints for inverse DF nonnegative. Hard.

Maybe use nonnegative matrix factorization: Represent q as average of two sequences? If q = r*s? Not.

Consider taking q_i = b_i b_{n-1-i} where b are nonnegative? Then q*q may have structure? Not.

If q_i = weights of discrete sine transform? For sequence q_i = 1/√(2n-1)?? Let's test a simple U-shape: q_i ∝ 1/√(min(i+1,n-i))? The convolution of edge-singular might flatten. We can grid search α in initialization and then local optimize.

Could use simulated annealing/random restarts: Since L-BFGS local, multiple starts. Our time can optimize top 3 starts. Need ensure diversity. Parent likely near optimum; optimizing parent with L-BFGS may slightly improve. Random restarts could find better but less time.

Maybe use **deterministic annealing in frequency square projection**: Starting from flat target sqrt, iterative projection onto nonnegative time support and sum constraint can converge to a good solution without local L-BFGS. Let's explore this method more; it may be a game-changer.

Alternating projections:
- Set 1: time domain: q length n, q_i>=0, sum=1.
- Set 2: square target: q*q (zero-padded) coefficients are close to a desired convex set? We can choose C = q*q and impose C_k = flat (all equal) or bounds. The set of q such that q*q has a specified C is nonconvex. But projection via sqrt FFT approximates set 2.
Algorithm:
```
q = initial (maybe uniform)
for iter:
   c = conv(q,q)
   c_target = projection(c, constraints: maybe all coefficients <=T and sum=1)
   q = project_time(sqrt_freq(c_target))
```
Where `project_time` clips negative, zeros beyond n, normalize.
If target set is `c_target = c` modified by hard peak clipping: c_k = min(c_k, T) then normalize. This is like "peak picking".

Let's implement `softmax` helper:
```
def softmax_vec(z):
    z = z - np.max(z)
    exp = np.exp(z)
    s = exp.sum()
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s): return np.ones(len(z))/len(z)
    return exp/s
```

def box_sqrt_optimize(q0, time_limit, target_score=1.50, rng=None):
    q=normalize(q0); n; best=q; best_sc=score
    start=time; M0=score/(2n)
    T = M0 * 0.995
    # target lower maybe max(target_score/(2n), 0.05*M0?)
    T_min = target_score/(2*n)
    # If M0 <= T_min, T=M0*0.995 else start M0*0.99
    iter_count=0
    while time<deadline and T > T_min*0.95:
       for blend in [0.5,1.0,0.25]: # maybe full if improve
          cand = box_sqrt_step(q, T)
          if valid:
             sc=score(cand)
             if sc < best_sc: best,cand...
       # reduce T by factor 0.97 or if no improvements
       T = max(T_min, T*0.97)
    # Also try target_score fixed
```

### Water fill box function
Implement efficiently with sorting to avoid while many:
Given c normalized sum1, threshold T. Want minimize? Water fill as described: cap at T, add to below uniformly. Algorithm:
```
def water_fill_box(c, T):
    c = np.maximum(c,0)
    s=c.sum()
    if s<=0: return c
    c = c/s
    if c.max() <= T:
       return c
    # We can sort c ascending.
    # Start with all values; high values capped T. Remaining to distribute among values that after adding <=T.
    # Equivalent final values: x_i = min(max(c_i, ?), T)? Let's derive.
    # If we cap high at T and add uniform δ to entries below T, entries with c_i + δ may exceed T; then they also fixed at T, reducing δ. This is standard.
    # Sort ascending c. Let sorted a. We'll find cutoff index m of entries below T after fill. For m entries lowest, final sum = sum(a[:m]) + m*δ, with δ=(1 - (M-m)*T)/m? Wait final: top M-m entries fixed at T if their original c >? Actually all entries with final T are high; count h = M-m. Sum high = h*T. Sum low = total 1 - h*T. For low entries count m, uniform add gives each (total - sum(c_low))/m, but they must be <=T; and original high entries should be >=T. Need choose m such that average of low +? Let's find.
```

```
out = np.minimum(c, T)
while True:
   rem = 1.0 - out.sum()
   if rem <= 1e-15: break
   low_mask = out < T
   cnt = low_mask.sum()
   if cnt == 0: break
   add = rem / cnt
   if out[low_mask].max() + add <= T:
       out[low_mask] += add
       break

### `make_initializations`
```
def make_initializations(n, rng, parent):
  inits=[]
  # parent resampled
  if parent is not None and parent.size>0:
      p=resample_to_length(parent,n)
      p=normalize_nonneg(p)
      if p is not None: inits.append(p)
  # uniform
  inits.append(np.ones(n)/n)
  # U shapes
  idx=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
  e=0.5
  left=idx+e; right=n-idx+e
  for a in (0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0):
      q=np.power(left,-a)*np.power(right,-a)
      q=normalize_nonneg(q); if ok inits.append(q)
  # shifted/asymmetric U
  for a,b in [(0.75,0.25), (0.25,0.75), (1.0,0.5), (0.5,1.0)]:
      q = np.power(left,-a)*np.power(right,-b); ...
  # cosine edge
  x=idx/(n-1)
  q=np.maximum(0.1+0.9*np.abs(2*x-1),1e-3); normalize
  q=np.maximum(0.2+0.8*np.cos(np.pi*x)**2,1e-3); normalize
  # two-tone edge (maybe q = (1+0.5*cos(2πx))**2)
  # random smooth
  for trial in range(3):
      q=np.ones(n)
      for f in range(1,6):
          amp=0.05*rng.random(); ph=rng.uniform(0,2π)
          q += amp*np.cos(2π*f*idx/n + ph)
      q=normalize; if ok
  # sqrt flat projection from uniform? Can't call inside? We can generate later rather than here to avoid heavy.
  return inits
```
Need avoid duplicates; not critical.
`normalize_nonneg`:
```
def normalize_nonneg(a, min_floor=1e-12):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float).ravel()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a, min_floor)  # floor to keep positive? But if want zeros? For optimization, positive. For output allow 0. Use floor 1e-12.
    s=a.sum()
    if not finite or s<1e-12: return None
    a/=s
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    return a
```
For initializations, maybe floor changes zeros; okay.

### `resample_to_length`
```
def resample_to_length(a, n_new):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel(); m=len(a)
    if m==0: return None
    if m==n_new: return a.copy()
    x_old=np.arange(m,dtype=float)
    x_new=np.linspace(0, m-1, n_new)
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
```
Maybe use `np.linspace(0,1,n_new)` and old `arange(m)/(m-1)`. same.

### LSE objective function details
Need pass `n` to avoid len? But can use len(z). However `fast_conv` uses len. Use `n = len(z)`. Function:
```
def lse_softmax_objective(z, beta):
    n = len(z)
    zc = z - np.mean(z)
    q = softmax_vec(zc)
    c = fast_conv(q)
    # c length 2n-1
    r = float(np.max(c))
    scaled = beta*(c - r)
    expv = np.exp(scaled)
    expsum = float(expv.sum())
    if expsum <= 1e-300 or not finite:
        val = 1e100; grad=np.zeros(n); return val, grad
    u = expv / expsum
    L = r + np.log(expsum)/beta
    val = 2.0*n*L
    # corr via FFT
    Mconv = 3*n - 2
    L2 = 1 << (Mconv - 1).bit_length()
    Q = np.fft.rfft(q, L2)
    Ur = np.fft.rfft(u[::-1], L2)
    full = np.fft.irfft(Q * Ur, L2)
    corr = full[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    avg = float(np.dot(u,c))
    grad = 2.0 * q * (corr - avg)  # for L (not multiplied by 2n?)
    # If val=2nL, gradient wrt z = 2n * (2q(corr-avg)) = 4n... Wait grad above =2q(corr-avg) for L. Multiply by 2n -> 4n. Return with factor.
    grad *= 4.0 * n
    grad -= np.mean(grad)
    if not finite: return 1e100,zeros
    return val, grad
```
Check: `fast_conv` returns length 2n-1 for zero-padded convolution of length n? For q normalized, sum c=1. Good.
Potential factor in val: `L = r + log(expsum)/beta`. If beta large. val=2nL. But LSE approximate (1/beta) logsumexp = r+.../beta. Good.
Gradient for L wrt z = 2 q(corr - avg), where corr uses u. Let's verify h=2corr? In `corr` we computed ∑ u_k q_{k-j}. Derivative of L wrt q = ∑ u_k * 2 q_{k-j} = 2corr. Sum q_i derivative =2avg. Thus dL/dz = q(2corr -2avg)=2q(corr-avg). Yes. Times 2n.

L2 next pow 4. full[t]=∑ q_s u_rev[t-s]. We want corr_0 = q0 u0 + q1 u1? Since j=0, k=0..1? Wait r from 0 to n-1=1, corr_0 = ∑_{r=0}^1 q_r u_{0+r}=q0u0+q1u1. corr_1 = q0u1+q1u2. ### Multiplicative descent details implementation
```
def multiplicative_descent(q0, n_iters=1000, beta0=5000.0, beta_end=200000.0, step0=0.1, max_step=0.5, time_limit=None, report=0):
    start=time.monotonic()
    q=normalize_nonneg(q0, min_floor=1e-14)
    n=len(q); z=np.log(q); z-=np.mean(z)
    best=q.copy(); best_sc=conv_score(q)
    eta=step0
    no_accept=0
    for it in range(n_iters):
       if time_limit is not None and time.monotonic()>time_limit: break
       progress=it/max(1,n_iters)
       beta=beta0*(beta_end/beta0)**progress if beta_end>beta0 else beta0
       c=fast_conv(q)
       M=max(c); sc=2nM
       # if sc < best update
       scaled=beta*(c-M)
       # prevent overflow? scaled <=0, but if beta huge and c-M ~ -1e4, exp underflow fine.
       expv=np.exp(scaled)
       s=expv.sum()
       if s<=0 or not finite: break
       u=expv/s
       Mconv=3*n-2; L2=...
       Q=rfft(q,L2); Ur=rfft(u[::-1],L2); full=irfft; corr=...
       avg=dot(u,c)
       d = q*(corr - avg)
       d -= np.mean(d)
       dn = norm(d)
       if dn<1e-14: break
       dir = -d / dn  # direction in z to decrease? Wait d positive for high contributors; want z -= step*d/dn. So dir = -d/dn and z_new=z+step*dir.
       accepted=False
       trial=eta
       for _ in range(5):
          if trial<1e-8: break
          z_new = z + trial*dir
          q_new=softmax_vec(z_new)
          sc_new=conv_score(q_new)
          if sc_new < sc - 1e-13:  # strict
             z=z_new; q=q_new; sc=sc_new; accepted=True
             if sc<best_sc: best_sc=sc; best=q.copy()
             break
          trial *= 0.5
       if accepted:
          no_accept=0
          η maybe increase if trial < eta? But trial unknown. Set eta = min(max_step, eta*1.03)
       else:
          no_accept +=1
          eta *= 0.5
          if no_accept > 30:
             # try random perturbation? maybe decrease beta? Or break? 
             # We can perturb z with small noise and continue
             z += 0.02*rng.standard_normal(n)? Need rng; pass rng. If not, use normal global? We can pass rng.
             no_accept=0
    return best,best_sc
```
Need pass rng to multiplicative for perturbation? We can add `rng` parameter.
But each line search calls `conv_score` which does FFT; 5 per iter. Could be expensive. Maybe use direct score from c before direction? For line search, need new c. Could reduce to 3 tries. Also we can accept if `sc_new < best_sc` even if not < current? For descent need current. Use 6.
Step values: z-scale maybe 0.05-0.5. For small direction, 0.2 can change q significantly. We'll start 0.1.

But with `d` normalized, if d captures gradient well, line search can accept. If not, eta shrinks.

Potential issue: The objective `conv_score` uses max; changes may be nonmonotone, line search may reject small improvements due noise. Use tolerance `sc_new < sc` with 1e-15. For LSE weights, direction may not exactly decrease max, but LSE smooth; check exact score. Maybe accepting based on LSE value would be smoother. We can compute LSE value for new q with same beta cheaper? It requires conv plus exp/dot; similar. But exact max is goal. Could accept if exact score <= sc + 1e-16? Maybe for local moves not monotone exact but good for LSE. We can allow small increase if LSE improved, but keep best. Simpler: exact only.

Alternative: Use gradient direction on LSE but project by multiplicative update with fixed small eta and periodically check; if score worsens, rollback. Might be okay.

def amp_flat_optimize(q0, n_iters=1000, alpha_start=0.05, alpha_end=0.8, time_limit=None):

    for it in range(n_iters):

       progress=...
       alpha=...
       A=fft(q,L); amp=abs; phase=angle
       C=np.median(amp[1:]); C=max(C,1e-20)
       amp_new=amp*(C/amp)**alpha
       A_new=amp_new*exp(1j*phase)
       q_new=irfft(A_new,L)[:n]
       q_new=normalize_nonneg(q_new)
       # blend with q to avoid jump: t maybe 1, if score improves else 0.5,0.1
       for blend in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1]:
           cand=(1-blend)*q + blend*q_new
           cand=normalize_nonneg(cand)
           sc=conv_score(cand)
           if sc<best_sc and sc<conv_score(q)? update best? Need current score.
           if sc < current_sc: q=cand; current_sc=sc; if sc<best_sc update; break

    return best

def box_sqrt_optimize(q0, time_limit, target_score=1.5027, rng=None):
    q=normalize; best=q; best_sc=score; n=len(q)
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=time_limit
    M0=best_sc/(2*n)
    T=min(M0*0.995, max(target_score/(2*n), 1e-12))
    if T >= M0: T=M0*0.9
    T_lower=max(target_score/(2*n)*0.999, M0*0.5)
    # If target lower > M0, just current.
    no_improve=0
    while time<deadline:
       step_q = None
       if uniform random >0.3:
           cand = box_sqrt_step(q, T)
       else:
           # power target
           # maybe skip
       for blend in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1]:
           candb=(1-blend)*q+blend*step_q; candb=normalize; sc=score
           if sc < best_sc: best...; if sc < current: q=candb; current=sc; no_improve=0; break
       if no_improve: T = max(T_lower, T*0.97); no_improve+=1
       else: T = max(T_lower, T*0.995)
    return best
```

### `conv_score` maybe if S=1, no issues.
```
def conv_score(q):
   q=np.asarray...
   if not finite: return inf
   q=np.maximum(q,0)
   S=q.sum()
   if S<1e-15: return inf
   c=fast_conv(q/S)
   c=np.maximum(c,0)
   return 2*len(q)*float(c.max())
```
Since we normalize externally, can avoid S division but robust.

### Refining box sqrt step symmetrization
Let's implement full FFT sym robustly:
```
def sqrt_target_to_sequence(q, target, n):
    L = 1 << (2*n-2?).bit_length()
    # target length 2n-1. Pad to L automatically by fft.
    Cf = np.fft.fft(target, L)
    # ensure tiny imaginary? target real, Cf Hermitian.
    Bf = np.sqrt(Cf)
    # Force Hermitian by averaging with conjugate reversed:
    inv = np.arange(L)
    Bf = 0.5*(Bf + np.conj(Bf[(-inv) % L]))
    Bf[0] = float(np.abs(Bf[0]))
    if L % 2 == 0:
        Bf[L//2] = float(np.abs(Bf[L//2]))
    b = np.fft.ifft(Bf).real
    b = np.maximum(b[:n], 0.0)
    s=b.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12: return None
    return b/s
```
But full FFT for L=32768 maybe heavier but okay for few calls. Use `np.fft.fft` complex length L; Bf complex. `(-inv)%L` creates array length L, okay. Optimize: For rfft, maybe better. But full robust.
Potential issue: The symmetrization changes DC/Nyquist magnitude; after sym, DC maybe still 1. We set. For Nyq, if Bf[Nyq] after averaging has small imaginary; set real abs. But if Bf[Nyq] due negative Cf had imaginary, averaging with conj? Nyq is self-conj index; `Bf[Nyq] = 0.5*(Bf[Nyq] + conj(Bf[Nyq]))` makes real (Re), if original i, becomes 0. Then abs. This drops. Good.
However taking `Bf = sqrt(Cf)` principal branch: For Cf zeros and noise, Bf may have random half-phase; sym averaging can cancel. Could produce b sum not 1 but normalized.
This projection may be somewhat random in branch; can we choose branch to maximize sum of first n? The square has two roots ±Bf; time sequence sign flip; sum can be negative. We choose DC positive; sign fixed. Could also multiply Bf by -1 globally; same sum neg. Not useful. Could randomize phases of Bf? The square root of each DFT has two choices ±; to keep Hermitian, signs for -k pair must be both flipped? Random sign choices per bin could find better real/nonnegative b, like phase retrieval. We could try a few random sign flips:
```
sgn = np.ones(L, float); sgn = rng.choice([-1,1], L); enforce Hermitian sgn[k]=sgn[-k], DC=1, Nyq=1; Bf *= sgn
```
But flipping sign for a pair squares to +, so still sqrt of Cf? If sgn ±1, Bf^2 unchanged (since (-B)^2=B^2) for that bin; yes. In time domain, random sign choices change b while preserving target C exactly in frequency (circular) up to sym. This is powerful! For real b, need sgn Hermitian: sgn_k = sgn_{L-k}; for self bins ±1. Then Bf real? If Cf has complex phase, Bf complex; multiplying by ± real preserves Hermitian. We can try a few random sign assignments to improve b positivity/support. But full random L signs too many. We can choose random signs for each non-self frequency. But 32768 bits; one random vector cheap. This is like `time-domain phase retrieval`. It may help. But if target C arbitrary, any sign choices yield a sequence b whose circular square equals C. We project first n/nonneg. Different signs may yield more nonnegative. This could use to initialize.

for t in range(n_trials):

   if t>0:

### Let's refine `water_fill_box`
```
def water_fill_box(c, T):
    c = np.maximum(np.asarray(c,float),0)
    s = float(c.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300: return c
    c = c / s
    T = float(T)
    if T <= 0: return c
    if c.max() <= T:
        return c
    out = np.minimum(c, T)
    for _ in range(20):
        rem = 1.0 - float(out.sum())
        if rem <= 1e-12:
            break
        low = out < T
        cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(low))
        if cnt <= 0:
            break
        add = rem / cnt
        if add <= 0:
            break
        # if the largest low plus add <= T, done
        max_low = float(out[low].max()) if cnt else 0.0
        if max_low + add <= T:
            out[low] += add
            break
        # fix those that would exceed T
        threshold = T - add
        fix = (out > threshold) & (out < T)
        if not fix.any():
            # numerical: fix the largest low
            fix_idx = np.argmax(np.where(low, out, -np.inf))
            fix = np.zeros_like(out, bool); fix[fix_idx]=True
        out[fix] = T
    out = np.maximum(out,0)
    s2=out.sum()
    if s2>0: out/=s2
    return out
```
If T is very small, may need many iterations? at most number of distinct levels. OK.

### For `box_sqrt_step`, target T must be at least average. If T < 1/M, infeasible; set T=max(T,1/M*1.001). For target score lower bound 1.0 -> T~0.5/n above avg 0.5/(2n)? Actually avg=1/(2n-1)~0.5/n. Score target 1.5 -> T=0.75/n > avg. Good.
If current max less than target T, no need box.

### `box_sqrt_optimize` time

def box_sqrt_optimize(q0, time_limit, target_score=1.5027, rng=np.random.default_rng(0)):
    q=normalize(q0); best=q; best_sc=conv_score(q); n=len(q); M=2*n-1
    deadline=time_limit
    M0=best_sc/(2*n)
    T_lower=max(target_score/(2*n), 0.9/(M))? Since target max 1.502/(2n). Lower bound not use.
    T=min(M0*0.995, T_lower)
    if T < 1/M: T=1.001/M
    if T >= M0: T = M0*0.9
    no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        base = box_sqrt_step(q,T)
        # Maybe also try current score exact: if base None skip
        improved_step=False
        if base is not None:
            for blend in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1):
                cand=(1-blend)*q + blend*base
                cand=normalize(cand)
                sc=conv_score(cand)
                if sc < best_sc:
                    best=cand; best_sc=sc
                if sc < conv_score(q) or sc < best_sc? For update current if improves current:
                    q=cand; improved_step=True; break
        if improved_step:
            no_improve=0
            T = max(T_lower, T*0.999)
        else:
            no_improve +=1
            T = max(T_lower, T*0.98)
            if no_improve > 20: # random perturbation
                q = normalize(q*(1+0.05*rng.standard_normal(n)))
                no_improve=0
    return best
```
But calling `conv_score(q)` inside blend each time could be repeated; store current_sc. Use variable. Need minimize calls. `box_sqrt_step` does c and sqrt; each blend cand score does conv. At most 4 scores per step. Could be heavy but okay if few.
Maybe use `blend = 1.0` only; if no improve, try 0.5/0.25. We can implement.

### Let's outline final code in more detail.

```
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time

def fft_pow2(M): return 1 << (M-1).bit_length()
```

Helper `is_valid_sequence(a)`.

`fast_conv(a)`:
```
def fast_conv(a):
    n = len(a)
    M = 2*n - 1
    if n < 50:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    L = 1 << (M - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    return np.fft.irfft(A * A, L)[:M]
```
If a contains tiny negative due numerical, clip outside.

`normalize_nonneg(a, floor=0.0)`:
```
def normalize_nonneg(a, floor=0.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0: return None
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    if floor > 0:
        a = np.maximum(a, floor)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-15: return None
    a = a / s
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return a
```
If floor=1e-12, sum increases slight but normalizes.

`conv_score(a)`:
```
def conv_score(a):
    q=normalize_nonneg(a)        # uses floor=0; if none inf
    if q is None: return np.inf
    c=fast_conv(q)
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    return 2.0*len(q)*float(np.max(c))
```
Since normalize floors? floor=0, so zeros possible. For LSE, need positive.

`softmax_vec(z)`:
```
def softmax_vec(z):
    z = np.asarray(z,float)
    z = z - np.max(z)
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = e.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: return np.ones(len(z))/len(z)
    return e/s
```

`lse_softmax_objective(z, beta)`: as above. Use `n = len(z)`. Ensure `beta` float. At end if gradient has nonfinite: return finite large, zeros.
Potential performance: `full = np.fft.irfft(Q * Ur, L2)` where Q, Ur rfft. If L2 huge, okay. Need `Mconv = 3*n - 2`; L2 = 1 << (Mconv - 1).bit_length(). For n=16384, Mconv=49150, L2=65536.

`lbfgs_stage(q0, beta, time_limit, est_cost=None, maxfun_cap=3000)`:
```
def lbfgs_stage(q0, beta, time_limit, est_cost=None, maxfun_cap=3000):
    q=normalize_nonneg(q0, floor=1e-14)
    if q is None: return q0
    z=np.log(q); z-=np.mean(z)
    if est_cost is None:
        t=time.monotonic(); lse_softmax_objective(z,beta); est_cost=max(1e-5, time.monotonic()-t)
    remaining = max(1.0, time_limit - time.monotonic())
    maxfun = int(remaining / (est_cost * 2.0))
    maxfun = min(maxfun_cap, max(30, maxfun))
    maxiter = min(500, maxfun)
    try:
       res = minimize(lse_softmax_objective, z, args=(beta,), method='L-BFGS', jac=True, options={'maxiter':maxiter, 'maxfun':maxfun, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-7, 'maxcor':20, 'maxls':20})
       z_out=np.asarray(res.x,float)
       if np.any(~np.isfinite(z_out)): return best of q? maybe q
       q_out=softmax_vec(z_out - z_out.mean())
       sc0=conv_score(q); sc1=conv_score(q_out)
       if sc1 < sc0: return q_out
       return q
    except: return q
```
But `conv_score` twice; okay. We should also keep best maybe if q_out worse but intermediate better? no.
If q0 already not positive? floor.

`multiplicative_descent(q0, n_iters, beta0, beta_end, step0, time_limit, rng)`:
Need use `normalize_nonneg(q0, floor=1e-14)`, `z=log`. In line search, `softmax_vec`. Use `fast_conv`/score. Pass `rng` for random kick if no accept.

```
def multiplicative_descent(...):
    ...
    no_accept=0
    for it:
       if time_limit: break
       progress = it / max(1,n_iters)
       beta = beta0 * (beta_end/beta0)**progress
       c = fast_conv(q); M=...; sc=...
       if sc<best_sc: update
       if sc <= 1.001? break? no
       scaled=beta*(c-M)
       # if beta large, c-M min ~-M; min scaled ~-beta*M. If beta*M < -700, exp underflow; okay. If beta*M >? scaled <=0.
       expv = np.exp(scaled)
       s=expv.sum()
       if s<=0: use top exact
       else u=...
       Mconv=3*n-2; L2...
       corr = ... (maybe direct if only top? But u dense; FFT)
       avg = float(np.dot(u,c))
       d=q*(corr-avg); d-=np.mean(d); dn=norm(d)
       if dn<1e-14: break
       dir=-d/dn
       accepted=False
       trial=eta
       for _ in range(6):
           z_new=z+trial*dir
           q_new=softmax_vec(z_new)
           sc_new=conv_score(q_new)
           if sc_new < sc - 1e-15:
               z=z_new; q=q_new; sc=sc_new; accepted=True
               if sc<best_sc: update
               break
           trial*=0.5
       if accepted:
           no_accept=0
           if trial > eta*0.9: eta = min(max_step, eta*1.05)
           else eta = min(max_step, eta*1.02)
       else:
           no_accept +=1
           eta *=0.5
           if no_accept>40:
              # random small kick
              z += 0.05 * (rng.standard_normal(n) - np.mean(rng.standard_normal(n)))
              no_accept=0
              # recompute current sc?
    return best, best_sc
```
But the direction `dir=-d/dn`; if d normalized, small. Good.
Maybe best to accept if `sc_new < current_sc + 1e-14` and LSE improved? But exact only. If line search rejects all, eta may become tiny. Random kick helps.

`random_sqrt_init(n, rng, n_trials=6, target='flat')`:
Implement as described. Need `water_fill_box` maybe for box? For flat target.
```
def random_sqrt_init(n, rng, n_trials=6):
  M=2*n-1; L=1<<(M-1).bit_length()
  target=np.ones(M)/M
  Cf=np.fft.fft(target,L)
  Bf=np.sqrt(Cf)
  # symmetrize once?
  inv=np.arange(L)
  Bf=0.5*(Bf + np.conj(Bf[(-inv)%L]))
  Bf[0]=abs(Bf[0])
  if L%2==0: Bf[L//2]=abs(Bf[L//2])
  best=None; best_sc=inf
  for t in range(n_trials):
     if t==0: signs=np.ones(L)
     else:
        signs=np.ones(L)
        half=L//2
        signs_half=rng.choice(np.array([-1.0,1.0]), size=half)
        idx=np.arange(1, half+1)
        signs[idx]=signs_half
        signs[L-idx]=signs_half
        if L%2==0: signs[half]=1.0
     b=np.fft.ifft(Bf*signs).real
     cand=np.maximum(b[:n],0.0); s=cand.sum()
     if s>1e-6:
        cand/=s; sc=conv_score(cand); update
  if best is None: best=np.ones(n)/n
  return best
```
But is `Bf*signs` for each trial; signs length L, multiplication cheap. For t=0, signs all ones. Good.
Need be careful: Multiplying Bf by random signs preserves Hermitian if signs Hermitian. We set signs[L-idx]=signs[idx]; for DC index0=1; Nyq=1. Good.
Could also try target T from a box with T=1.5? Not needed.

`box_sqrt_step(q, T)`:
```
def box_sqrt_step(q, T):
   n=len(q)
   c=fast_conv(q); s=c.sum(); if s<=0 return None; c=c/s
   T=max(T, 1.001/(2*n-1))
   target=water_fill_box(c,T)
   L=1<<... 
   Cf=np.fft.fft(target,L)
   Bf=np.sqrt(Cf)
   inv=np.arange(L)
   Bf=0.5*(Bf + np.conj(Bf[(-inv)%L]))
   Bf[0]=abs(Bf[0]); if L%2==0: Bf[L//2]=abs(Bf[L//2])
   b=np.fft.ifft(Bf).real
   cand=np.maximum(b[:n],0); s=cand.sum(); if s<=1e-6: return None
   return cand/s
```
This uses full FFT each call. Could be called many; okay.

`box_sqrt_optimize(q0, time_limit, target_score, rng)`:
Use while; time_limit is absolute monotonic? Pass absolute deadline. Need current_sc variable. Use normalizes.
```
def box_sqrt_optimize(q0, deadline, target_score=1.5027, rng=np.random.default_rng(1)):
   q=normalize(q0, floor=1e-14); best=q; best_sc=conv_score(q); current_sc=best_sc; n=len(q)
   M=2*n-1
   M0=best_sc/(2*n)
   T_lower=max(target_score/(2*n), 1.001/M)
   T=min(M0*0.995, max(T_lower, 0.9*M0))
   if T >= M0: T=M0*0.9
   if T < 1.001/M: T=1.001/M
   no_improve=0
   trial_count=0
   while time.monotonic() < deadline:
      trial_count +=1
      base=box_sqrt_step(q,T)
      if base is None:
         T=max(T_lower,T*0.95); continue
      improved_current=False
      for blend in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1):
         cand=(1-blend)*q + blend*base
         cand=normalize_nonneg(cand)
         if cand is None: continue
         sc=conv_score(cand)
         if sc < best_sc:
             best_sc=sc; best=cand.copy()
         if sc < current_sc - 1e-14:
             q=cand.copy(); current_sc=sc; improved_current=True; break
      if improved_current:
         no_improve=0
         T=max(T_lower, T*0.999)
      else:
         no_improve+=1
         T=max(T_lower, T*0.97)
         if no_improve>15:
            q=perturb...
            current_sc=conv_score(q)
            no_improve=0
   return best,best_sc
```
Need `deadline` as absolute time. `rng` used.

`amp_flat_optimize(q0, deadline, alpha_start, alpha_end)`:
```
def amp_flat_optimize(q0, deadline, alpha_start=0.05, alpha_end=0.8):
   q=normalize; best; current=sc; n; L=...
   no_improve=0
   alpha=alpha_start
   while time<deadline:
      A=np.fft.rfft(q,L); amp=np.abs(A); phase=np.angle(A)
      if len(amp)>1: C=np.median(amp[1:]); C=max(C,1e-20)
      else C=amp[0]
      amp_safe=np.maximum(amp,1e-20)
      amp_new=amp_safe*(C/amp_safe)**alpha
      A_new=amp_new*np.exp(1j*phase)
      q_new=np.fft.irfft(A_new,L)[:n]
      q_new=normalize_nonneg(q_new)
      if q_new is None: break
      improved=False
      for blend in (1.0,0.5,0.25):
         cand=(1-blend)*q + blend*q_new
         cand=normalize_nonneg(cand)
         sc=conv_score(cand)
         if sc<best_sc: update best
         if sc<current_sc-1e-14:
            q=cand; current_sc=sc; improved=True; break
      if improved:
         no_improve=0
         alpha=min(alpha_end, alpha*1.1)
      else:
         no_improve+=1
         alpha=max(alpha_start*0.25, alpha*0.7)
         if no_improve>40: break
   return best,best_sc
```
This may be many FFTs. Use only if time.

```
def improve_sequence(q0, total_time, rng):
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+total_time-5
    q=normalize(q0, floor=1e-14); best=q; best_sc=conv_score(q); current=q; current_sc=best_sc
    if total_time < 30: return current,best_sc
    est_cost=estimate_lse_cost(len(q))
    # Phase 1: L-BFGS anneal with chunks
    phase1_end = start + min(0.45*total_time, deadline-start)
    betas = [2000, 8000, 30000, 100000, 300000, 1000000]
    chunk = (phase1_end - start) / len(betas)
    for beta in betas:
       if time.monotonic() > phase1_end: break
       t_stage = min(phase1_end, time.monotonic()+chunk)
       q_out = lbfgs_stage(current, beta, t_stage, est_cost, maxfun_cap=2000)
       sc_out = conv_score(q_out)
       if sc_out < current_sc - 1e-12:
           current=q_out; current_sc=sc_out
       if sc_out < best_sc: best=sc; best=q_out
       # quick multiplicative within remaining chunk
       t_multi = min(phase1_end, time.monotonic()+max(1.0, 0.3*chunk))
       q_mult, sc_mult = multiplicative_descent(current, n_iters=5000, beta0=max(500,beta//2), beta_end=min(5e6,beta*20), step0=0.08, time_limit=t_multi, rng=rng)
       if sc_mult < current_sc: current=q_mult; current_sc=sc_mult
       if sc_mult < best_sc: best...
    # Phase 2: long multiplicative exact
    phase2_end = min(deadline, start+0.75*total_time)
    if time < phase2_end:
       q_mult, sc = multiplicative_descent(current, n_iters=100000, beta0=max(1000, best_sc? no), beta_end=2e6, step0=0.05, time_limit=phase2_end, rng=rng)
       update
    # Phase 3: escape methods
    phase3_end = min(deadline, start+0.9*total_time)
    if time < phase3_end:
       # box sqrt
       q_box, sc = box_sqrt_optimize(current, phase3_end*0.6, target_score=1.5027, rng=rng.copy())
       update
       # amp flatten if time
       t_amp = min(phase3_end, time+ (phase3_end - time)*0.6)
       q_amp,sc=amp_flat_optimize(current,t_amp,...); update
    # Phase 4: final L-BFGS high beta and multiplicative
    if time < deadline:
       q_out=lbfgs_stage(current, 2e6, deadline, est_cost, maxfun_cap=3000); update
       q_mult, sc = multiplicative_descent(current, ..., time_limit=deadline, rng=rng); update
    # Return best (not current if current worse)
    if conv_score(current) < best_sc? best already
    return best
```

### Let's think if there is a more efficient **multiplicative update** derived from KKT that directly converges faster.
For objective max p, at optimum for positive q, there exist weights u (sum1) on active peaks such that gradient condition: 2 q_j (corr_j - avg) = 0? Actually objective F=2n max. For weighted max, optimum of smooth surrogate satisfies maybe corr_j = constant for all positive j? Because dL/dz=0 implies q_j(corr_j-avg)=0. For all positive, corr_j=avg. That means for active weight u, `∑_k u_k q_{k-j}` is constant over j. This is like u* (shift) q flat. Could solve by multiplicative update:
```
q_new_j ∝ q_j / (corr_j/avg) ?
```
Fienup style: Set new q proportional to q / corr (where corr is current peak-feedback), because to make corr constant. Specifically, update `q_j *= (corr_avg / corr_j)^alpha`. This is like amplitude correction. This is very efficient: no L-BFGS, iterate. Let's derive.
Given weights u on peaks, define feedback S_j = corr_j = ∑ u_k q_{k-j}. We want S_j constant. Multiplicative update in time: q_j^new = q_j * (C / S_j)^α, C maybe median(S) or avg. Then normalize. This is direct. It may be effective. Let's include `feedback_multiplicative`:
- Compute c, u LSE weights (top peaks), corr.
- Set `q_new = q * (C/np.maximum(corr, eps))**alpha`; normalize.
This is analogous to Fienup but in time domain from convolution feedback. Since S_j has scale avg~1/n, C can be median(corr) or max? We want high S_j (variables contributing to peaks) reduced. Use C = median(corr[finite]) or max? If use `C = np.median(corr)`, q_new proportional to q*(med/corr)^α. This pushes S toward constant? Multiplicative: new corr? Not guaranteed but common. This is much faster and maybe effective. We can use as primary optimizer instead of L-BFGS? Let's test concept near uniform. If peaks central, corr_j high for j central? Update lowers central q, raises edges? Good.

```

### Let's refine `lbfgs_stage` to use current `est_cost` and not double estimate each call.
```
def lbfgs_stage(q0, beta, time_limit, est_cost, maxfun_cap=2500):
    q = normalize_nonneg(q0, floor=1e-14)
    if q is None: return q0
    z = np.log(q); z -= np.mean(z)
    remaining = max(0.5, float(time_limit - time.monotonic()))
    # Estimate eval time includes two FFTs? objective cost maybe. Use conservative.
    maxfun = int(remaining / (est_cost * 1.5))
    maxfun = int(np.clip(maxfun, 30, maxfun_cap))
    maxiter = int(np.clip(maxfun, 30, 500))
    try:
       res = minimize(...)
       z_out = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(z_out)): return q
       z_out -= np.mean(z_out)
       q_out = softmax_vec(z_out)
       sc0 = conv_score(q); sc1=conv_score(q_out)
       if sc1 < sc0: return q_out
       return q
    except Exception:
       return q
```

### `estimate_lse_cost(n, beta=20000)`

def estimate_lse_cost(n, beta=20000.0):
    z=np.zeros(n)
    t=time.monotonic()
    lse_softmax_objective(z,beta)
    dt=time.monotonic()-t
    return max(1e-5, dt)
```

### `feedback_descent` in detail
```
def feedback_descent(q0, time_limit, beta_min=1000.0, beta_max=1000000.0, alpha_min=0.2, alpha_max=1.0, rng=None):
    q = normalize_nonneg(q0, floor=1e-14)
    if q is None: return None, np.inf
    n=len(q); best=q.copy(); best_sc=conv_score(q); current_sc=best_sc
    start=time.monotonic(); duration=max(1e-6, time_limit-start)
    alpha = (alpha_min+alpha_max)/2? use start alpha_min
    no_accept=0
    while time.monotonic() < time_limit:
       progress = min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-start)/duration)
       if beta_max > beta_min:
           beta = beta_min * (beta_max/beta_min) ** progress
       else: beta=beta_min
       alpha = alpha_min + (alpha_max-alpha_min)*progress
       # adjust alpha based on no_accept
       if no_accept > 0:
           alpha = min(alpha_max, max(alpha_min, alpha * (0.7 if no_accept<20 else 1.3)))
       c = fast_conv(q); c=np.maximum(c,0); s=c.sum(); if s>0 c/=s
       M = float(np.max(c))
       # if M <= 1.001/(2n-1): near lower bound break?
       if M <= 1.002/(2*n-1): break
       scaled = beta*(c-M)
       expv = np.exp(scaled)
       s_exp = expv.sum()
       if not finite or s_exp <=0: break
       u = expv/s_exp
       # corr
       Mconv=3*n-2; L2=1 << (Mconv -1).bit_length()
       Q=np.fft.rfft(q,L2); Ur=np.fft.rfft(u[::-1],L2); full=np.fft.irfft(Q*Ur,L2); corr=full[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
       pos = corr > 1e-300
       if pos.any(): C = float(np.median(corr[pos]))
       else: C = float(np.max(corr))
       C = max(C, 1e-300)
       eps = 1e-300
       factor = (C / np.maximum(corr, eps)) ** alpha
       factor = np.clip(factor, 1e-4, 1e4)
       q_raw = q * factor
       q_raw = normalize_nonneg(q_raw)
       if q_raw is None: break
       accepted=False
       for blend in (1.0,0.75,0.5,0.25,0.1):
           cand = (1-blend)*q + blend*q_raw
           cand=normalize_nonneg(cand)
           if cand is None: continue
           sc=conv_score(cand)
           if sc < best_sc:
               best_sc=sc; best=cand.copy()
           if sc < current_sc - 1e-14:
               q=cand.copy(); current_sc=sc; accepted=True; break
       if accepted:
           no_accept=0
       else:
           no_accept +=1
           if no_accept > 30 and rng is not None:
               # random noise kick from best
               q = normalize_nonneg(best + 0.05*best*rng.standard_normal(n), floor=1e-14)
               if q is None: break
               current_sc=conv_score(q)
               no_accept=0
           if no_accept > 100: break
    return best, best_sc
```
Potentially `conv_score` called many times; line search 5. Maybe too slow. We can reduce blends to 3: (1.0,0.5,0.25). Use 3.
Also if `c` normalized each iteration, conv_score also normalizes; okay.
Potential bug: `c/=s` for q sum1; if s not 1 due floor, okay.

Should C be median or avg? We use median positive. Could be unstable if median very small. Alternative use C = max(np.median(corr[pos]), 0.5*float(np.dot(q, corr))). Let's use target C = `0.5 * (median + avg)`? We can compute avgcorr = float(np.dot(q, corr)) (which equals dot(u,c)). Then C = max(median, 0.3*avgcorr, 1e-300). This prevents too small. If median < avg, C between. Let's do:
```
avg_corr = float(np.dot(q, corr))
med_corr = float(np.median(corr[pos])) if pos.any() else avg_corr
C = max(0.5 * (med_corr + avg_corr), 1e-300)
```
This targets halfway. But if median=0, C=0.5avg. Good. Could also use just avg. We'll set `C = avg_corr`? The feedback factor with C=avg is gradient-like. If corr_j often 0 (many q small), factor huge for zeros clipped; can increase many small variables. Maybe median safer. Use `C = 0.5*(med+avg)`.
Line search protects.

`avg_corr` could be computed efficiently as `float(np.dot(u,c))` same avg p. Use that; no dot q corr. In peak feedback, `avg = dot(u,c)`. Since corr scale? `dot(q,corr)=dot(u,c) = avg`. So C = 0.5*(med+avg). Good.

### `improve_sequence` using feedback and LBFGS in chronological order
```
def improve_sequence(q0, total_time, rng):
    start=time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(10.0, total_time - 5.0)
    q=normalize_nonneg(q0, floor=1e-14)
    if q is None: return q0
    current=q.copy(); current_sc=conv_score(q); best=q.copy(); best_sc=current_sc
    n=len(q)
    est=estimate_lse_cost(n)
    def? no.
    # Phase 1 feedback broad
    if time.monotonic() < deadline:
        phase_end = min(deadline, start + 0.25*total_time)
        newq,newsc = feedback_descent(current, phase_end, beta_min=200, beta_max=20000, alpha_min=0.1, alpha_max=0.7, rng=rng)
        update
    # Phase 2 LBFGS mid
    if time.monotonic() < deadline:
       phase_end = min(deadline, start + 0.45*total_time)
       # maybe run two betas 5000 and 20000
       chunk=(phase_end - time.monotonic())
       for beta in [5000, 20000, 80000]:
           if time > phase_end: break
           t_stage = min(phase_end, time.monotonic()+max(5, chunk/3?))
           newq=lbfgs_stage(current,beta,t_stage,est)
           sc=conv_score(newq); update
    # Phase 3 feedback sharp
    if time<deadline:
       phase_end = min(deadline, start + 0.65*total_time)
       newq,newsc=feedback_descent(current, phase_end, beta_min=10000, beta_max=500000, alpha_min=0.3, alpha_max=1.0, rng=rng)
       update
    # Phase 4 LBFGS high
    if time<deadline:
       phase_end = min(deadline, start + 0.80*total_time)
       for beta in [100000, 400000, 1500000]:
          if time>phase_end: break
          t_stage = min(phase_end, time.monotonic()+max(5, (phase_end-time.monotonic())/3))
          newq=lbfgs_stage(current,beta,t_stage,est)
          update
    # Phase 5 escape + final
    if time<deadline:
       phase_end = min(deadline, start + 0.95*total_time)
       # box sqrt for half
       t_box = min(phase_end, time.monotonic() + max(5, 0.5*(phase_end-time.monotonic())))
       newq,newsc=box_sqrt_optimize(current, t_box, target_score=1.5027, rng=rng.copy())
       update
       # amp flat if time
       if time.monotonic() < phase_end:
          newq,newsc=amp_flat_optimize(current, phase_end*0.9? absolute)
          update
    # final feedback very short if time
    if time.monotonic() < deadline:
       newq,newsc=feedback_descent(current, deadline, beta_min=100000, beta_max=2000000, alpha_min=0.1, alpha_max=0.8, rng=rng)
       update
    return best, best_sc
```

#### `fast_conv`

#### `corr` indexing in `lse_softmax_objective`
Same as feedback. For n=3, Mconv=7, L2=8. full length8. corr=full[2:5][::-1] length3. We tested. Good.

#### `feedback_descent` C median positive
If `corr` has zeros many, `pos` median of positives. If only one positive, median=that; avg maybe small; C = 0.5*(single+avg). Factor for that positive maybe ~ (0.5(s+a))/s <1 if a<s, reduce. Zeros get large factor clipped 1e4; could increase tiny variables, line search maybe accept? It might increase many variables, rapid. Clip 1e4 maybe too high; use 100? #### `water_fill_box` with T maybe > current max? returns normalized c. If target T very low, iteration. Good.

#### `box_sqrt_step` full FFT sym
`inv = np.arange(L)`, `Bf[(-inv) % L]` indexing array shift. For L large, okay. Then `Bf[0]=abs(Bf[0])`; if L even `Bf[L//2]=abs(Bf[L//2])`. But after averaging, Nyquist may be complex? `0.5*(Bf[Nyq] + conj(Bf[Nyq]))` real. abs.
Then `np.fft.ifft(Bf).real`. Good.
If first n sum small, return None.

def make_initializations(n, rng, parent):

        p=resample...; if p: inits.append(p)

    return inits

def safe_score_sequence(seq_list):

def water_fill_box(c, T):
    c = np.maximum(np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64), 0.0)
    s = float(c.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return c
    c = c / s
    T = float(max(T, 0.0))
    if T <= 0.0:
        return c
    if float(c.max()) <= T:
        return c
    out = np.minimum(c, T)
    for _ in range(30):
        rem = 1.0 - float(out.sum())
        if rem <= 1e-12:
            break
        low = out < T
        cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(low))
        if cnt <= 0:
            break
        add = rem / cnt
        if add <= 0:
            break
        max_low = float(out[low].max())
        if max_low + add <= T:
            out[low] += add
            break
        threshold = T - add
        fix = (out > threshold) & (out < T)
        if not np.any(fix):
            # choose largest low
            low_idx = np.where(low)[0]
            if low_idx.size == 0:
                break
            idx = int(low_idx[np.argmax(out[lowIdx])])
            fix = np.zeros_like(out, dtype=bool)
            fix[idx] = True
        out[fix] = T
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    s2 = float(out.sum())
    if s2 > 0.0:
        out = out / s2
    return out
